## Playbooks

### INV-ECON-0040 `building_investment_capital_preflight_drift`

1. Read fatal_context_report()'s investment row: branch (1 = drained sponsor, 2 = capital top-up), cell, type_id, portfolio_index/portfolio_size.
2. Compare carried and correction against required_capital: branch 1 fails when carried < required_capital, branch 2 when sponsor_funds < correction.
3. Check the investment_legs trace per leg (allocated, carried, correction, credit, owner_funds_at_material); a leg whose carried claim vanished points at move_cohort_population, a short sponsor pool points at find_entrepreneur_source.
4. Re-run the failing day with the economy invariant probe fixture for the same cell/type before touching ledger order or funding rules.
5. Regression: python tools/ci/check_fault_taxonomy.py --strict plus the economy invariant probe suite, and keep the flat investment_row keys in fatal_context_report().

### INV-ECON-0041 `building_construction_preflight_stock_drift`

1. Read fatal_context_report()'s construction row: cell, type_id, first_good_id, first_required, first_stock, good_count.
2. The commit loop failed on stock[market, good] < planned_quantities[i]; compare the preflight material plan (good_ids/quantities) with the lane stock read at commit.
3. Look for a same-epoch consumer of the same (market, good) lane between preflight and commit (production input purchase, maintenance, army lane pay) and for merchant restock ordering.
4. Re-run the day with the probe fixture for that cell; do not tolerate negative stock or clip the plan to hide the drift.
5. Regression: python tools/ci/check_fault_taxonomy.py --strict plus building commit tests.

### INV-ECON-0042 `building_construction_preflight_funds_drift`

1. Read fatal_context_report()'s construction row: owner_slot, owner_funds, total_cost, funding_gap, debt_principal, material_feasible.
2. Recompute the preflight projection for that owner: funds at plan time must cover total_cost; a mismatch means funds moved between preflight and invoice (tax settlement, subsidy, another construction leg).
3. Check the funding legs that were supposed to cover the invoice: funding_capital + merchant_credit >= total_cost, and whether material_plan.feasible flipped after the plan was built.
4. Re-run with the economy invariant probe fixture for that cell/type; do not relax the preflight or fall back to silently skipping the build.
5. Regression: python tools/ci/check_fault_taxonomy.py --strict plus the economy suite, keeping the flat construction_row keys.

### INV-SAVE-0001 `save_family_expedition_person_invalid`

1. Read the restore rejection reason reported by set_restore_rejected_reason() together with the PKEC family-expedition section cursor: the person lane of one expedition payload was rejected.
2. The assertion has two triggers: a serialized person handle of 0, or a live slot whose cohort handle is non-zero / family handle differs from the expedition family. A dead handle is already dropped into _restore.dropped_transit_persons, so a stop here is the attribution mismatch case.
3. Dump the save's person lane and the restored persons_store() rows for that handle: cohort_handle, family_handle, and the expedition family, then decide whether the lane is orphaned or the family attribution is wrong.
4. Fix the producing side (who wrote a person into the expedition lane) or, when the lane is genuinely orphaned, drop it explicitly and count it; never zero the lane silently.
5. Regression: PKEC save/restore round-trip test plus python tools/ci/check_fault_taxonomy.py --strict.
